      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Discover capabilities in the current session\n\nRead the tools exposed to this session before assigning work. Cached official-subagents.txt is documentation context; it proves no operation succeeded. Local agent configuration is not portable plugin configuration.\n\nRecord each binding as `{operation, tool, exposed, succeeded, evidence, limits}`. `tool` is the exact exposed identifier or null when absent. `succeeded` is true only after observing a result. Keep failed and unavailable bindings. Successful bindings become `successfulOperations` in host evidence, with a root-relative evidence file. The operation labels below describe purposes, never callable APIs.\n\n| Operation | Discovery and observation required |\n| --- | --- |\n| spawn | Find an exposed independent-worker operation; retain returned worker identity and exact assigned revision. |\n| follow-up | Find the operation that sends input to that worker; observe acceptance by the same identity. |\n| wait | Find the operation that waits for worker state; retain terminal or interrupted state. |\n| inspect-result | Read the worker result and its referenced artifacts; a wait notification alone is insufficient. |\n| stop | Observe cancellation or termination through the exposed operation. |\n| read | Observe the requested file content through the available file tool. |\n| isolated-write | Observe a write within the worker's assigned path and permissions. |\n| history | Observe available prior turns or records; declare gaps explicitly. |\n| execution | Observe command execution with exit status and output. |\n| persistence | Write and read an authorized durable record; a claimed save is insufficient. |\n| external-write | Record explicit authorization and action identity; inspect the result before replay. |\n| resolve-ownership | Observe distinct owned paths before contested writes. |\n| inspect-terminal | Observe the worker's terminal state after stop. |\n| quarantine-artifacts | Observe partial artifacts withheld from integration. |\n| inspect-prior-action | Read the interrupted action identity and prior outcome. |\n| inspect-destination | Observe the destination state for that action identity. |\n| safe-recovery | Observe explicitly authorized idempotent recovery after identity and destination inspection. |\n\nNo default API names are assigned here. Tool names differ by host and session. An exposed tool may still be denied or fail. Record requested and actual models only when observed; otherwise use `unknown`. A requested model never proves the actual model.\n\nA denied or unavailable operation blocks the branch that requires it. Sequential substitution for required independent work is reduced capability and uses `reduced-pass`; ordinary direct completion can fully pass. Missing persistence limits session pickup even when current work succeeds. Preserve user authorization and host permission boundaries through every fallback.\n\nWorker passes require observed spawn and result inspection. Each registry entry declares `requiredObservations` for `pass` and `reduced-pass`; tool observations need exposed bindings and existing evidence artifacts; native text observations use the separate reviewed native record below. Full cancellation requires stop, terminal inspection and artifact quarantine. Full persistence requires durable write and readback. Full interrupted-action recovery requires prior identity inspection, destination inspection, safe recovery, explicit external permission and a recorded prior action identity even when no external-write observation is listed.\n\nPermission checks follow performed operations. Isolated writes and persistence require granted writes. External writes and safe recovery require granted external permission and prior identity. Authorized read-only reductions may retain denied writes; their limits must preserve denied or unavailable permissions. Required observations remain applicable to those reductions.\n\nHost evidence is stored as object or array JSON files under `docs/parity/evidence/`. Required fields are defined by `docs/parity/evidence.schema.json`. Artifacts and operation evidence must be existing files inside the tested root. Test fixtures belong in `tests/` and prove validator behavior only.\n\nRun `python3 tools/validate_evidence.py --root . --commit <tested-package-commit>` through Bun, for example `bun -e 'const p=Bun.spawnSync([\"python3\",\"-B\",\"tools/validate_evidence.py\",\"--root\",\".\",\"--commit\",process.argv[1]],{env:{...process.env,PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE:\"1\"}});process.stdout.write(p.stdout);process.stderr.write(p.stderr);process.exit(p.exitCode)' <tested-package-commit>`. It reads every JSON record below the evidence directory, prints each error, and exits 1 for invalid records or 0 for valid records. An empty directory has no invalid records and supplies no behavioral evidence.\n\nThe required registry file in `docs/parity/case-registry.json` declares applicability. Missing or non-file registry, unknown case, structural-only entry or unlisted host rejects behavioral validation. Maintenance fixtures use a temporary copy of this registry; they cannot bypass applicability. Required hosts must receive observed evidence or explicit blocked records before release claims. Structural checks have no required host and cannot populate host status. Later producers propose task-owned entries; only the coordinator integrates them. Source paths select callers through the task 3 case contract. Case definitions alone leave host statuses `not-run`.\n\n## Direct completion and recorded observations\n\n`direct` means the task is completed in the current context. Restatement, supplied-text writing and complete installed discovery can pass directly. `sequential` is reserved for replacing a required independent method with sequential work and remains reduced. A text response has no invented callable tool. Record supplied input, the actual response and qualitative outcome review as `nativeObservations`, each with its existing listed evidence artifact and named reviewer. Their labels are `supplied-input`, `response` and `review-outcome`. The reviewer assesses the case's expected outcome; validation checks evidence relationships and does not decide prose quality.\n\nEach registry entry declares allowed execution modes and required permissions for each status. A grant is required for the performed action: worker spawn, command execution and stop need execution; isolated output or persistence needs writes; external actions need their separate permission and prior identity. Read-only discovery does not inherit an execution grant requirement. Tool observations still name an actually exposed tool and existing evidence. Native observations cannot claim tool discovery, worker identity or saved writes.\n\nFull Arena and Swarm results additionally provide `inputRevision` and a listed `methodReceipt` artifact. An exposed `inspect-method` operation must reference that receipt. Assignment operations name `assignmentId` and the corresponding inspected output. See the [structured method contract](orchestration-contract.md). All positive maintenance fixtures are synthetic; none is an observed host result.\n"

      "text": "{\n  \"head\": {\n    \"args\": [\n      \"git\",\n      \"rev-parse\",\n      \"HEAD\"\n    ],\n    \"exitCode\": 0,\n    \"stdout\": \"95b349fda1654096baa379cd1e74a609cf5ae43f\\n\",\n    \"stderr\": \"\"\n  },\n  \"diff\": {\n    \"args\": [\n      \"git\",\n      \"diff\",\n      \"--exit-code\",\n      \"HEAD\",\n      \"--\"\n    ],\n    \"exitCode\": 0,\n    \"stdout\": \"\",\n    \"stderr\": \"\"\n  },\n  \"stagedDiff\": {\n    \"args\": [\n      \"git\",\n      \"diff\",\n      \"--cached\",\n      \"--exit-code\"\n    ],\n    \"exitCode\": 0,\n    \"stdout\": \"\",\n    \"stderr\": \"\"\n  },\n  \"status\": {\n    \"args\": [\n      \"git\",\n      \"status\",\n      \"--short\"\n    ],\n    \"exitCode\": 0,\n    \"stdout\": \"?? outputs/\\n\",\n    \"stderr\": \"\"\n  },\n  \"reportReadback\": true,\n  \"responseReadback\": true\n}\n"

    {
      "path": "input-gap-repairs/stale-feature-map/outputs/source-3.md",
      "bytes": 6273,
      "sha256": "5dd740ad66d992df252754d189d0dc207900023372392a0db4309b85eb5e4ec5",
      "owner": "local-013-repair-06-stale-feature-map",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Assignment and integration contract\n\nBefore dispatch, the coordinator creates an immutable assignment with `id`, `scope`, `inputRevision`, `outputOwner`, `isolatedOutputPath`, `role`, `retryCount`, `terminalStatus`, and `evidenceLinks`. Use the full Git SHA for inputRevision, an exact owned path, and an explicit role. Start retryCount at 0 and terminalStatus at `pending`. Preserve every attempt as an append-only event under the same assignment ID; update status through events, never rewrite the original scope, revision, owner or path. A changed scope or revision requires a new assignment ID that links its predecessor.\n\nThe coordinator checks authorization and observed host bindings before dispatch. One path has one writer. Reject overlapping paths before work starts; assign distinct paths instead. Workers write only their isolated output and report results. Only the coordinator integrates shared files, inventory, registry, or other workers' output.\n\nAccept a result only when its assignment identity and input SHA match, its artifacts exist, and required method evidence was observed. Reject stale revisions. Permit at most one retry for missing required SHA or method evidence, after inspecting the prior attempt and confirming the same permissions still apply. A retry count of 1 exhausts this allowance. Denied permissions, unknown action outcomes, and missing artifacts never become success by retry policy.\n\nTerminal states are `completed`, `blocked`, `failed`, and `cancelled`. A completion event links the exact result and evidence. Stop a cancelled worker through the exposed stop binding when available, then inspect its terminal state. Quarantine any partial artifacts; cancellation does not authorize their integration. If stop cannot be observed, record that limit and do not claim termination. Report interrupted execution explicitly.\n\nBefore an external action, record explicit permission, a stable action identity, the intended destination and the last observed outcome. After interruption, inspect whether that identity already completed. Retry only with authorization, an observed safe replay decision and an idempotency mechanism appropriate to the destination. If the outcome is unknown, block replay. Do not infer permission from upstream autonomy instructions.\n\nHistory and persistence have separate bindings. Keep assignment events and result links in an authorized durable location and verify readback. If unavailable, declare the gap and offer an authorized local handoff as reduced capability; never claim unattended recovery or cross-session continuity. All host statuses remain `not-run` until actual observations exist.\n\n## Structured Arena and Swarm receipts\n\nA method receipt is JSON with schemaVersion 1, kind `arena` or `swarm`, inputRevision, assignments, reviewArtifact, reviewedBy and declaredOrder. The inputRevision is the actual task repository revision, distinct from the packageCommit identifying these instructions. It must match the evidence record. The declared order precedes every dispatch. reviewArtifact records the qualitative outcome review and reviewedBy identifies its reviewer. All referenced artifacts must exist and be listed in the evidence record.\n\nEach assignment records id, workerId, inputRevision, outputOwner, outputPath, role, status, startedOrder, completedOrder, requestedModel, actualModel, retryCount, method, taskArtifact, rubricProvided, resultRevision, resultMethod and outputSha256. Result revision and method must match the assignment, and the inspected output bytes must match their digest. Worker and assignment identities are distinct. outputOwner equals workerId; output directories do not overlap. Integer order values record the observed dispatch and completion sequence, not estimated time. The method has positive sampleCount, sampleDefinition and order. Missing model observations use unknown. The validator requires assignment-bound spawn and result inspection, and isolated-write for output-producing candidates. A read-only judge may return its result through the coordinator's authorized artifact capture. A cancelled result needs bound stop, terminal inspection and quarantine observations. No operation string alone establishes a method.\n\nArena adds candidates, judge, rubricArtifact and synthesis. At least two candidates share the same taskArtifact, finish before a distinct fresh judge starts and do not receive the parent rubric. Synthesis has order, inspectedCandidates, artifact and verificationArtifact; it follows completed judgment and accounts for every candidate. The review artifact assesses scoring, base selection, grafts, rejections and verification quality. File presence and phase ordering do not establish the quality of those decisions.\n\nSwarm adds partition, race, cancelled and quarantined. Partition declares requiredSlices, results mapping each slice to its assignment, and gaps. A full coverage claim requires all slices with completed inspected outputs; gaps remain explicit reduced or blocked work. Race declares rule, assignments, winner and ruleArtifact. first-pass must select the first completed survivor; rank-all needs all results; best-of retains an explicit reviewed selection. Cancelled assignments must be quarantined and cannot enter the aggregate. The combined partition-and-race case requires both methods and its cancellation exercise. The partition walkthrough exercises only its declared slices; it does not acquire an unrelated race requirement.\n\nThe three-file Swarm walkthrough requires exactly three declared slices and three completed results. Its partition-only receipt uses race null and empty cancellation, quarantine and failureExercises arrays. The combined case also records failureExercises. Each event has assignmentId, kind, attempt, order, reason and evidence, with bound result-rejection or terminal inspection. Rejected missing-revision or missing-method evidence uses attempts 0 then 1; a second invalid result exhausts the retry and leaves a failed or blocked assignment. A separate failed or blocked dropout is disclosed. These failed outputs cannot satisfy required coverage. The final assignment result metadata records the inspected terminal summary; rejection-event artifacts retain the invalid attempts.\n"
    },

      "path": "input-gap-repairs/stale-feature-map/outputs/source-4.md",
      "bytes": 1265,
      "sha256": "50644b3a9a3dfd5651382eaca1d3a2728af0b3157d6d66b730930370a64bb03c",
      "owner": "local-013-repair-06-stale-feature-map",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Workflow ownership\n\nAn explicit pstack invocation gives pstack ownership of that workflow. An explicit Superpowers invocation gives Superpowers ownership. Do not run both controllers over the same assignment or let one silently replace the other's plan, review, or integration decisions.\n\nFor an unqualified request, D1 retains the already active workflow. With no active workflow, use host defaults and do not activate either orchestration mode automatically. If both pstack and Superpowers are explicitly requested, clarify ownership before launching workers or changing artifacts. Preserve the active controller across follow-ups unless the user changes ownership. A helper skill can advise the owner without becoming a second controller.\n\nThe owner assigns one coordinator. Workers produce isolated artifacts and evidence; the coordinator reviews and integrates them. Shared inventory and registry writes belong to the coordinator. Worker cancellation, stale results or unavailable tools do not transfer that ownership automatically.\n\nHost permissions and user authorization constrain every owner. Local structural tests, documented APIs and declared cases provide no host pass. Explicit reduced or blocked results remain visible beside the full expectation.\n"
    },

    {
      "path": "input-gap-repairs/stale-feature-map/outputs/source-1.md",
      "bytes": 6549,
      "sha256": "5b29cabb79d03a7817a3482a92f9054d817c833d58f4126167ccfedaebf54d7a",
      "owner": "local-013-repair-06-stale-feature-map",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "---\nname: maintain-verification-skill\ndescription: \"Periodic pass that keeps a project's verification skill and feature map honest: parallel source readers per feature, one live session driving every feature, at most one PR of proven corrections. Use for /maintain-verification-skill or \\\"audit the verify skill\\\".\"\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\n# Maintain a verification skill\n\nA feature map rots the moment the app changes. This skill is the upkeep loop for a skill generated by `/create-verification-skill` (or any project-local verification skill with a feature map). The unit of rigor is the feature, not every sentence: cover every feature file from source and exercise every feature live, without terminalising every bullet.\n\n## Host bindings and permissions\n\nRead [host capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md), [orchestration contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md), and [workflow ownership](../../references/workflow-ownership.md). Follow the active owner. Bind reads, independent workers, execution, app driving, writes and external actions to exposed tools and observed results. Documentation alone proves no capability.\n\nDiscover the current host's project-local skill location from repository conventions and exposed documentation. Do not assume a vendor-specific directory or registration. If the target cannot be found, follow step 0. If independent workers are unavailable, read every feature serially and report reduced capability; serial readers are not independent workers. Keep the same source and live proof standards. Follow the assignment contract for worker identity, revision, ownership, result inspection and retries. Children remain read-only.\n\nIf execution or app driving is denied or unavailable, retain source findings and unrun recipes, mark live verification `not-run` and report `blocked`. If writes are denied, report recommended corrections without editing. Installation, listeners, credentials, external writes and shipping require applicable authorization. Do not schedule recurring upkeep from this invocation. A changed supplied app contract needs source and live reconciliation; never silently lower the proof requirement to accommodate drift.\n\n## Outcomes\n\nPick one, and say which:\n\n- **clean**, every feature got source and live coverage; nothing worth shipping. No branch, no PR.\n- **changed**, one authorized PR ships proven doc, harness, or map corrections.\n- **blocked**, coverage could not finish or a proven fix could not ship safely. Say exactly what blocked it.\n\n## Edit scope\n\nOnly edit the verification skill's own directory (its SKILL.md, features/, and any harness scripts it owns). Never edit product code during a run: a behavior the map describes that the app no longer does is either doc drift (fix the map) or a product regression (report it, don't paper over it in docs).\n\n## Pass\n\n0. **Locate the target.** Find the verification skill to maintain: the project-local skill whose body has launch/drive sections and a feature map (in the observed project-local skill directory). Several candidates, ask which one; none, stop and point at `/create-verification-skill` instead of inventing a target.\n\n1. **Index hygiene.** Read the feature map README and glob its sibling files. Fix missing, extra, duplicate, or dead entries. Lightweight; no generated inventory.\n\n2. **Source wave.** One read-only subagent per feature file, launched concurrently. Each explains \"how does this user-facing feature work?\" from source, flags likely doc drift with citations, and returns one concise live-verification recipe. Children never drive the app and never edit files. Return shape: feature summary / source entry points / likely drift or none / one recipe.\n\n3. **Reconcile.** Every feature file has a returned summary. Merge overlapping recipes into as few app states as practical. Spot-check cited drift; don't re-prove clean claims. Sweep recent churn for user-facing surfaces missing from the map, require a concrete source path before calling one missing.\n\n4. **Live pass.** Required even when source looks clean. The coordinator owns all driving; follow the verification skill's own launch model, one long-lived instance driven serially for servers and UIs, or a fresh isolated session per drive for short-lived CLIs (the skill's Launch section decides, not this one). Exercise every feature at least once, and hold three invariants the whole pass, whatever the failure: (1) never drive an instance you haven't health-checked since it last did something surprising, doctor before first drive, doctor on each fresh session where sessions are the unit, doctor again after any failed drive, and where doctor can't see the failure (a wedged UI state on a healthy process), reset to a known state or relaunch rather than hoping; (2) evidence captured so far survives every cleanup, checked at its named location, not assumed; (3) nothing a drive started outlives that drive's usefulness, failed-iteration residue is cleaned whether the session is stuck, exited, or shared (for a shared instance, clean the residue, not the instance). A doctor failure caused by skill drift is drift: fix it under edit scope and retry once, restart whatever the fix invalidated, nothing more, before calling the pass `blocked`. A feature that can't be reached is `verified-unreachable` only with the concrete prerequisite (auth, entitlement, OS, external state) and the route attempted; if the map omits that prerequisite, that's drift. Any harness fix from triage gets re-driven live before it ships. Final teardown happens after the last drive of the run, including those re-proofs, so nothing outlives the run (evidence stays, per the skill).\n\n5. **Triage.** Fix wrong or missing user-POV descriptions as doc drift. Fix working behavior the harness can't drive as a harness gap. Harness fixes follow the same helpers rule as generation: scripts are executable and the skill body documents their invocation. Record broken app behavior as a product gap for the user and keep it out of this PR.\n\n6. **Ship or stop.** For changed: re-read every changed file first, then ship one PR of proven corrections only with explicit authorization. Without shipping authorization, retain the proven corrections for local review and report blocked shipping. Do not create a branch or PR. For clean or blocked: no PR, report the outcome and the coverage honestly.\n\nKeep concise run notes (features covered, unreachable prerequisites, confirmed drift, outcome) in a scratch location; don't commit them.\n"

This skill is the upkeep loop for a skill generated by `/create-verification-skill` (or any project-local verification skill with a feature map). The unit of rigor is the feature, not every sentence: cover every feature file from source and exercise every feature live, without terminalising every bullet.\\n\\n## Host bindings and permissions\\n\\nRead [host capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md), [orchestration contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md), and [workflow ownership](../../references/workflow-ownership.md). Follow the active owner. Bind reads, independent workers, execution, app driving, writes and external actions to exposed tools and observed results. Documentation alone proves no capability.\\n\\nDiscover the current host's project-local skill location from repository conventions and exposed documentation. Do not assume a vendor-specific directory or registration. If the target cannot be found, follow step 0. If independent workers are unavailable, read every feature serially and report reduced capability; serial readers are not independent workers. Keep the same source and live proof standards. Follow the assignment contract for worker identity, revision, ownership, result inspection and retries. Children remain read-only.\\n\\nIf execution or app driving is denied or unavailable, retain source findings and unrun recipes, mark live verification `not-run` and report `blocked`. If writes are denied, report recommended corrections without editing. Installation, listeners, credentials, external writes and shipping require applicable authorization. A changed supplied app contract needs source and live reconciliation; never silently lower the proof requirement to accommodate drift.\\n\\n## Outcomes\\n\\nPick one, and say which:\\n\\n- **clean**, every feature got source and live coverage; nothing worth shipping. No branch, no PR.\\n- **changed**, one authorized PR ships proven doc, harness, or map corrections.\\n- **blocked**, coverage could not finish or a proven fix could not ship safely. Say exactly what blocked it.\\n\\n## Edit scope\\n\\nOnly edit the verification skill's own directory (its SKILL.md, features/, and any harness scripts it owns). Several candidates, ask which one; none, stop and point at `/create-verification-skill` instead of inventing a target.\\n\\n1. **Index hygiene.** Read the feature map README and glob its sibling files. Fix missing, extra, duplicate, or dead entries. Lightweight; no generated inventory.\\n\\n2. **Source wave.** One read-only subagent per feature file, launched concurrently. Each explains \\\"how does this user-facing feature work?\\\" from source, flags likely doc drift with citations, and returns one concise live-verification recipe. Children never drive the app and never edit files. Return shape: feature summary / source entry points / likely drift or none / one recipe.\\n\\n3. **Reconcile.** Every feature file has a returned summary. Merge overlapping recipes into as few app states as practical. Spot-check cited drift; don't re-prove clean claims. Sweep recent churn for user-facing surfaces missing from the map, require a concrete source path before calling one missing.\\n\\n4. **Live pass.** Required even when source looks clean. The coordinator owns all driving; follow the verification skill's own launch model, one long-lived instance driven serially for servers and UIs, or a fresh isolated session per drive for short-lived CLIs (the skill's Launch section decides, not this one). Exercise every feature at least once, and hold three invariants the whole pass, whatever the failure: (1) never drive an instance you haven't health-checked since it last did something surprising, doctor before first drive, doctor on each fresh session where sessions are the unit, doctor again after any failed drive, and where doctor can't see the failure (a wedged UI state on a healthy process), reset to a known state or relaunch rather than hoping; (2) evidence captured so far survives every cleanup, checked at its named location, not assumed; (3) nothing a drive started outlives that drive's usefulness, failed-iteration residue is cleaned whether the session is stuck, exited, or shared (for a shared instance, clean the residue, not the instance). A doctor failure caused by skill drift is drift: fix it under edit scope and retry once, restart whatever the fix invalidated, nothing more, before calling the pass `blocked`. A feature that can't be reached is `verified-unreachable` only with the concrete prerequisite (auth, entitlement, OS, external state) and the route attempted; if the map omits that prerequisite, that's drift. Any harness fix from triage gets re-driven live before it ships. Final teardown happens after the last drive of the run, including those re-proofs, so nothing outlives the run (evidence stays, per the skill).\\n\\n5. **Triage.** Fix wrong or missing user-POV descriptions as doc drift. Fix working behavior the harness can't drive as a harness gap. Harness fixes follow the same helpers rule as generation: scripts are executable and the skill body documents their invocation. Record broken app behavior as a product gap for the user and keep it out of this PR.\\n\\n6. **Ship or stop.** For changed: re-read every changed file first, then ship one PR of proven corrections only with explicit authorization. Without shipping authorization, retain the proven corrections for local review and report blocked shipping. Do not create a branch or PR. For clean or blocked: no PR, report the outcome and the coverage honestly.\\n\\nKeep concise run notes (features covered, unreachable prerequisites, confirmed drift, outcome) in a scratch location; don't commit them.\\n\",\"exit_code\":0,\"status\":\"completed\"}}\n{\"type\":\"item.completed\",\"item\":{\"id\":\"item_6\",\"type\":\"agent_message\",\"text\":\"I’m applying the exact installed 0.1.3 `maintain-verification-skill` source. It requires stopping if no real target exists; I’ll read its capability and ownership rules, then check the supplied inputs.\"}}\n{\"type\":\"item.started\",\"item\":{\"id\":\"item_7\",\"type\":\"command_execution\",\"command\":\"/bin/zsh -lc \\\"cat /Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/references/host-capabilities.md /Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/references/orchestration-contract.md /Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/references/workflow-ownership.md\\nrg --files --hidden -g '\\\"'!.git'\\\"' -g '\\\"'!outputs'\\\"' .\\ncat README.md\\n git rev-parse HEAD\\n git status --short\\\"\",\"aggregated_output\":\"\",\"exit_code\":null,\"status\":\"in_progress\"}}\n{\"type\":\"item.completed\",\"item\":{\"id\":\"item_7\",\"type\":\"command_execution\",\"command\":\"/bin/zsh -lc \\\"cat /Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/references/host-capabilities.md /Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/references/orchestration-contract.md /Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/references/workflow-ownership.md\\nrg --files --hidden -g '\\\"'!.git'\\\"' -g '\\\"'!outputs'\\\"' .\\ncat README.md\\n git rev-parse HEAD\\n git status --short\\\"\",\"aggregated_output\":\"# Discover capabilities in the current session\\n\\nRead the tools exposed to this session before assigning work. Cached official-subagents.txt is documentation context; it proves no operation succeeded. Local agent configuration is not portable plugin configuration.\\n\\nRecord each binding as `{operation, tool, exposed, succeeded, evidence, limits}`. `tool` is the exact exposed identifier or null when absent. `succeeded` is true only after observing a result. Keep failed and unavailable bindings. Successful bindings become `successfulOperations` in host evidence, with a root-relative evidence file. The operation labels below describe purposes, never callable APIs.\\n\\n| Operation | Discovery and observation required |\\n| --- | --- |\\n| spawn | Find an exposed independent-worker operation; retain returned worker identity and exact assigned revision. |\\n| follow-up | Find the operation that sends input to that worker; observe acceptance by the same identity. |\\n| wait | Find the operation that waits for worker state; retain terminal or interrupted state. |\\n| inspect-result | Read the worker result and its referenced artifacts; a wait notification alone is insufficient. |\\n| stop | Observe cancellation or termination through the exposed operation. |\\n| read | Observe the requested file content through the available file tool. |\\n| isolated-write | Observe a write within the worker's assigned path and permissions. |\\n| history | Observe available prior turns or records; declare gaps explicitly. |\\n| execution | Observe command execution with exit status and output. |\\n| persistence | Write and read an authorized durable record; a claimed save is insufficient. |\\n| external-write | Record explicit authorization and action identity; inspect the result before replay. |\\n| resolve-ownership | Observe distinct owned paths before contested writes. |\\n| inspect-terminal | Observe the worker's terminal state after stop. |\\n| quarantine-artifacts | Observe partial artifacts withheld from integration. |\\n| inspect-prior-action | Read the interrupted action identity and prior outcome. |\\n| inspect-destination | Observe the destination state for that action identity. |\\n| safe-recovery | Observe explicitly authorized idempotent recovery after identity and destination inspection. |\\n\\nNo default API names are assigned here. Tool names differ by host and session. An exposed tool may still be denied or fail. Record requested and actual models only when observed; otherwise use `unknown`. A requested model never proves the actual model.\\n\\nA denied or unavailable operation blocks the branch that requires it. Sequential substitution for required independent work is reduced capability and uses `reduced-pass`; ordinary direct completion can fully pass. Missing persistence limits session pickup even when current work succeeds. Preserve user authorization and host permission boundaries through every fallback.\\n\\nWorker passes require observed spawn and result inspection. Each registry entry declares `requiredObservations` for `pass` and `reduced-pass`; tool observations need exposed bindings and existing evidence artifacts; native text observations use the separate reviewed native record below. Full cancellation requires stop, terminal inspection and artifact quarantine. Full persistence requires durable write and readback. Full interrupted-action recovery requires prior identity inspection, destination inspection, safe recovery, explicit external permission and a recorded prior action identity even when no external-write observation is listed.\\n\\nPermission checks follow performed operations. Isolated writes and persistence require granted writes. External writes and safe recovery require granted external permission and prior identity. Authorized read-only reductions may retain denied writes; their limits must preserve denied or unavailable permissions. Required observations remain applicable to those reductions.\\n\\nHost evidence is stored as object or array JSON files under `docs/parity/evidence/`. Required fields are defined by `docs/parity/evidence.schema.json`. Artifacts and operation evidence must be existing files inside the tested root. Test fixtures belong in `tests/` and prove validator behavior only.\\n\\nRun `python3 tools/validate_evidence.py --root . --commit <tested-package-commit>` through Bun, for example `bun -e 'const p=Bun.spawnSync([\\\"python3\\\",\\\"-B\\\",\\\"tools/validate_evidence.py\\\",\\\"--root\\\",\\\".\\\",\\\"--commit\\\",process.argv[1]],{env:{...process.env,PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE:\\\"1\\\"}});process.stdout.write(p.stdout);process.stderr.write(p.stderr);process.exit(p.exitCode)' <tested-package-commit>`. It reads every JSON record below the evidence directory, prints each error, and exits 1 for invalid records or 0 for valid records. An empty directory has no invalid records and supplies no behavioral evidence.\\n\\nThe required registry file in `docs/parity/case-registry.json` declares applicability. Missing or non-file registry, unknown case, structural-only entry or unlisted host rejects behavioral validation. Maintenance fixtures use a temporary copy of this registry; they cannot bypass applicability. Required hosts must receive observed evidence or explicit blocked records before release claims. Structural checks have no required host and cannot populate host status. Later producers propose task-owned entries; only the coordinator integrates them. Source paths select callers through the task 3 case contract. Case definitions alone leave host statuses `not-run`.\\n\\n## Direct completion and recorded observations\\n\\n`direct` means the task is completed in the current context. Restatement, supplied-text writing and complete installed discovery can pass directly. `sequential` is reserved for replacing a required independent method with sequential work and remains reduced. A text response has no invented callable tool. Record supplied input, the actual response and qualitative outcome review as `nativeObservations`, each with its existing listed evidence artifact and named reviewer. Their labels are `supplied-input`, `response` and `review-outcome`. The reviewer assesses the case's expected outcome; validation checks evidence relationships and does not decide prose quality.\\n\\nEach registry entry declares allowed execution modes and required permissions for each status. A grant is required for the performed action: worker spawn, command execution and stop need execution; isolated output or persistence needs writes; external actions need their separate permission and prior identity. Read-only discovery does not inherit an execution grant requirement. Tool observations still name an actually exposed tool and existing evidence. Native observations cannot claim tool discovery, worker identity or saved writes.\\n\\nFull Arena and Swarm results additionally provide `inputRevision` and a listed `methodReceipt` artifact. An exposed `inspect-method` operation must reference that receipt. Assignment operations name `assignmentId` and the corresponding inspected output. See the [structured method contract](orchestration-contract.md). All positive maintenance fixtures are synthetic; none is an observed host result.\\n# Assignment and integration contract\\n\\nBefore dispatch, the coordinator creates an immutable assignment with `id`, `scope`, `inputRevision`, `outputOwner`, `isolatedOutputPath`, `role`, `retryCount`, `terminalStatus`, and `evidenceLinks`. Use the full Git SHA for inputRevision, an exact owned path, and an explicit role. Start retryCount at 0 and terminalStatus at `pending`. Preserve every attempt as an append-only event under the same assignment ID; update status through events, never rewrite the original scope, revision, owner or path. A changed scope or revision requires a new assignment ID that links its predecessor.\\n\\nThe coordinator checks authorization and observed host bindings before dispatch. One path has one writer. Reject overlapping paths before work starts; assign distinct paths instead. Workers write only their isolated output and report results. Only the coordinator integrates shared files, inventory, registry, or other workers' output.\\n\\nAccept a result only when its assignment identity and input SHA match, its artifacts exist, and required method evidence was observed. Reject stale revisions. Permit at most one retry for missing required SHA or method evidence, after inspecting the prior attempt and confirming the same permissions still apply. A retry count of 1 exhausts this allowance. Denied permissions, unknown action outcomes, and missing artifacts never become success by retry policy.\\n\\nTerminal states are `completed`, `blocked`, `failed`, and `cancelled`. A completion event links the exact result and evidence. Stop a cancelled worker through the exposed stop binding when available, then inspect its terminal state. Quarantine any partial artifacts; cancellation does not authorize their integration. If stop cannot be observed, record that limit and do not claim termination. Report interrupted execution explicitly.\\n\\nBefore an external action, record explicit permission, a stable action identity, the intended destination and the last observed outcome. After interruption, inspect whether that identity already completed. Retry only with authorization, an observed safe replay decision and an idempotency mechanism appropriate to the destination. If the outcome is unknown, block replay. Do not infer permission from upstream autonomy instructions.\\n\\nHistory and persistence have separate bindings. Keep assignment events and result links in an authorized durable location and verify readback. If unavailable, declare the gap and offer an authorized local handoff as reduced capability; never claim unattended recovery or cross-session continuity. All host statuses remain `not-run` until actual observations exist.\\n\\n## Structured Arena and Swarm receipts\\n\\nA method receipt is JSON with schemaVersion 1, kind `arena` or `swarm`, inputRevision, assignments, reviewArtifact, reviewedBy and declaredOrder. The inputRevision is the actual task repository revision, distinct from the packageCommit identifying these instructions. It must match the evidence record. The declared order precedes every dispatch. reviewArtifact records the qualitative outcome review and reviewedBy identifies its reviewer. All referenced artifacts must exist and be listed in the evidence record.\\n\\nEach assignment records id, workerId, inputRevision, outputOwner, outputPath, role, status, startedOrder, completedOrder, requestedModel, actualModel, retryCount, method, taskArtifact, rubricProvided, resultRevision, resultMethod and outputSha256. Result revision and method must match the assignment, and the inspected output bytes must match their digest. Worker and assignment identities are distinct. outputOwner equals workerId; output directories do not overlap. Integer order values record the observed dispatch and completion sequence, not estimated time. The method has positive sampleCount, sampleDefinition and order. Missing model observations use unknown. The validator requires assignment-bound spawn and result inspection, and isolated-write for output-producing candidates. A read-only judge may return its result through the coordinator's authorized artifact capture. A cancelled result needs bound stop, terminal inspection and quarantine observations. No operation string alone establishes a method.\\n\\nArena adds candidates, judge, rubricArtifact and synthesis. At least two candidates share the same taskArtifact, finish before a distinct fresh judge starts and do not receive the parent rubric. Synthesis has order, inspectedCandidates, artifact and verificationArtifact; it follows completed judgment and accounts for every candidate. The review artifact assesses scoring, base selection, grafts, rejections and verification quality. File presence and phase ordering do not establish the quality of those decisions.\\n\\nSwarm adds partition, race, cancelled and quarantined. Partition declares requiredSlices, results mapping each slice to its assignment, and gaps. A full coverage claim requires all slices with completed inspected outputs; gaps remain explicit reduced or blocked work. Race declares rule, assignments, winner and ruleArtifact. first-pass must select the first completed survivor; rank-all needs all results; best-of retains an explicit reviewed selection. Cancelled assignments must be quarantined and cannot enter the aggregate. The combined partition-and-race case requires both methods and its cancellation exercise. The partition walkthrough exercises only its declared slices; it does not acquire an unrelated race requirement.\\n\\nThe three-file Swarm walkthrough requires exactly three declared slices and three completed results. Its partition-only receipt uses race null and empty cancellation, quarantine and failureExercises arrays. The combined case also records failureExercises. Each event has assignmentId, kind, attempt, order, reason and evidence, with bound result-rejection or terminal inspection. Rejected missing-revision or missing-method evidence uses attempts 0 then 1; a second invalid result exhausts the retry and leaves a failed or blocked assignment. A separate failed or blocked dropout is disclosed. These failed outputs cannot satisfy required coverage. The final assignment result metadata records the inspected terminal summary; rejection-event artifacts retain the invalid attempts.\\n# Workflow ownership\\n\\nAn explicit pstack invocation gives pstack ownership of that workflow. An explicit Superpowers invocation gives Superpowers ownership. Do not run both controllers over the same assignment or let one silently replace the other's plan, review, or integration decisions.\\n\\nFor an unqualified request, D1 retains the already active workflow. With no active workflow, use host defaults and do not activate either orchestration mode automatically. If both pstack and Superpowers are explicitly requested, clarify ownership before launching workers or changing artifacts. Preserve the active controller across follow-ups unless the user changes ownership. A helper skill can advise the owner without becoming a second controller.\\n\\nThe owner assigns one coordinator. Workers produce isolated artifacts and evidence; the coordinator reviews and integrates them. Shared inventory and registry writes belong to the coordinator. Worker cancellation, stale results or unavailable tools do not transfer that ownership automatically.\\n\\nHost permissions and user authorization constrain every owner. Local structural tests, documented APIs and declared cases provide no host pass. Explicit reduced or blocked results remain visible beside the full expectation.\\n./task-contract.json\\n./README.md\\n./committed-inputs/shared/additional/invoice/formatter.mjs\\n./committed-inputs/shared/additional/invoice/decision.md\\n./committed-inputs/shared/additional/invoice/controller.mjs\\n./committed-inputs/shared/additional/invoice/facade.mjs\\n./committed-inputs/shared/additional/invoice/adapter.mjs\\n./committed-inputs/shared/additional/invoice/exporter.mjs\\n./committed-inputs/shared/additional/invoice/calculator.mjs\\n./committed-inputs/shared/additional/legal-header/source.diff\\n./committed-inputs/shared/additional/legal-header/source.mjs\\n./committed-inputs/shared/additional/migration/notification.mjs\\n./committed-inputs/shared/additional/migration/contract.mjs\\n./committed-inputs/shared/additional/migration/api.mjs\\n./committed-inputs/shared/additional/migration/scope.md\\n./committed-inputs/shared/additional/migration/profile.mjs\\n./committed-inputs/shared/additional/history/jay-mode.md\\n./committed-inputs/shared/additional/history/capsule.json\\n./committed-inputs/shared/additional/history/shared-records.json\\n./committed-inputs/shared/additional/history/history.json\\n./committed-inputs/shared/additional/README.md\\n./committed-inputs/shared/additional/dead-path/instructions.md\\n./committed-inputs/shared/additional/dead-path/duplicate-reference.md\\n./committed-inputs/shared/additional/dead-path/handler.mjs\\n./committed-inputs/shared/additional/dead-path/caller.mjs\\n./committed-inputs/shared/additional/dead-path/scope.md\\n./committed-inputs/shared/additional/scenario-map.json\\n./committed-inputs/shared/additional/corpus/records.jsonl\\n./committed-inputs/shared/dashboard.mjs\\n./committed-inputs/shared/history.json\\n./committed-inputs/shared/orders.json\\n./committed-inputs/shared/invoice.mjs\\n./committed-inputs/shared/README.md\\n./committed-inputs/shared/forge.json\\n./committed-inputs/shared/additional/cache/invalidation.diff\\n./committed-inputs/shared/additional/cache/probe.mjs\\n./committe
